## Verdict after three rounds

Scores went from 3 to 5 in round 1 to 4 to 5 in rounds 2 and 3. The round 1 fixes (grounding, driver hardware, framing) moved the needle; rounds 2 and 3 asked for the same things on every shot: penumbra that widens with distance, paper fibre and ink grain, floor reflections, bounce light, and one light the whole frame obeys. Those are properties of a path-traced render or a photoreal pass, not of the real-time renderer in `render/`, which is the limit `CLAUDE.md` names. The geometry never came up: proportions, bowl, drivers and label were accepted as drawn in every round.

What the critic asked for that is still cheap and render-side, for the next camera pass: move the hero's left speaker off the room's corner line, add skirting and a larger floor to the desk set, keep the lifted lid off the horizon line in the swap, and offset the close-up camera a few degrees so the faceplate shows its edge.

What needs the photoreal pass: everything about surface and light. The frames in `renders/2026-10-01` are proportion-exact control images for it.

## Round 4, on the locked look (v4)

A fourth round because the look changed (printed lids on the white flavours, the band with the wordmark, nothing under the gable); the three-round cap counts per look. Scores held at 3 to 5. The new print was accepted without comment in every shot: no critic flagged the band, the lid colour or the wordmark position, which is the result that mattered for this round. The asks are the ones from rounds 2 and 3 (one committed light, paperboard, bounce, penumbra), which the real-time renderer does not do.

Cheap carries for v5, all camera or placement: hero fill down so the sun's shadows read; night key rotated off the hero face; close-up bowl with a soft terminator and the top lip out of frame; lineup from further back on a longer lens; crate camera higher and closer so the woofers stop reading as wheels, planks with the wall; the Whole lid moved clear of the standing tube in the swap's third frame.

## Round 5: not run

v5 carried the round 4 camera and placement fixes (hero fill down, night key raised, close-up terminator softened, lineup on a longer lens, crate camera higher, swap lid moved). Before the critic ran, the direction changed on 2026-10-02: no sleeve, the colour a fixed finish, the band and the stand one built-in plinth, the wordmark a badge on the front and engraved on the back, no owner's panel. The critic resumes on v6, the first render of that look.

## Round 1 on the finish look (v6)

The first round on the 2026-10-02 direction: no sleeve, the colour a satin finish, the plinth, the badge, the engraved back wordmark. Scores 4, 4, 4, 4, 5, 5. The new parts were accepted as the object: no critic questioned the plinth, the two-tone gable, or the engraved wordmark; two read the badge as a flat grey plate with unreadable type (01, 05), which v7 answers with a dark field inside a chrome rim and polished letters. The rest is the list from every earlier round, one light the whole frame obeys, contact occlusion, edge highlights, surface tooth, bounce, which is the real-time renderer's ceiling and waits for the photoreal pass.

Carries for the next camera pass: a plainer window in the two rooms so no mullion shadow crosses the hero face (02); a close-up crop that clears both the top lip and the mid's flange (03); a brief for shot 02 that is not the sleeve swap.

## Round 2 on the finish look (v7)

One change from v6: the badge became a chrome rim around a dark field with polished raised letters. Scores 4, 4, 4, 5, 5, 4. The badge now reads as a plate with lettering in the hero and the lineup; on Oat's dark plinth its dark field disappears into the plinth and only the rim shows, which is a question for the owner (a light field on the two dark flavours, or the rim alone). Everything else in the round is the renderer's ceiling restated.

Carries for the next camera pass, all small: toe the hero pair toward the lens and lower the camera; a plainer window in the two rooms with the night lamp's glow on the wall; a close-up crop that clears both the top lip and the mid's flange; the lineup a step further back; the crate camera a hand lower; and, if the owner agrees, the back panel's bottom lifted to z 140 so the panel keeps a white margin above the plinth like its sides.

## Round 3 on the finish look (v8)

One change from v7: the plate is gone and the wordmark is cast metal letters standing on the plinth. Scores 4, 5, 4, 4, 5, 4. No critic remarked on the lettering as a problem; one read it as debossed grey type, which is the pale studio environment reflected in polished metal rather than the letters themselves. Three rounds on this look have now said the same things on every shot, so the cap is reached: the remaining asks are one light the whole frame obeys, contact occlusion, edge highlights, surface tooth and bounce, all of which wait for the photoreal pass.

Still cheap and still open, for the next camera pass: toe the hero pair in on a longer lens from lower; remove the window mullion so no bar crosses the hero face in the two rooms, and brief that shot anew; a close-up crop that clears both the top lip and the mid's flange; the back camera lower so the seam leaves the Facts rule; the crate camera lower and the end-grain fringe on its base board fixed; a brighter environment behind the camera so the chrome letters read as chrome.

## v9 of the back: no critic round

v9 renders the contained birch field (the Facts panel plus a 26 mm margin, the wordmark engraved on the finish above, the port and posts on the finish below) as the new default, with the full field kept as an option; both are in `explore/2026-10-02/r4-*`. A spec choice for the owner rather than a render iteration, and the cap for this look is reached, so no round was run.

## v10 of the back: no critic round

v10 renders the back as decided on 2026-10-02: an engraved bronze plate for the Facts, the wordmark as cast letters like the front, SHAKE WELL under the port and OPEN OTHER SIDE on the back slope. Spec choices, and the cap for this look is reached, so no round was run.

## v11: the camera carries applied, no critic round

v11 applies the small notes the three rounds on the finish look agreed on: the hero pair toed in from a lower, longer camera; the window mullion gone from both rooms; the close-up aimed to clear the top lip and the mid's flange; the crate camera lower and its base board's end-grain fringe fixed. It also carries the owner's calls: SHAKE WELL on the plinth's back, and the Facts border enclosing its footnote with even padding. The cap for this look is reached, so no round was run; the next critic round waits for the photoreal pass or a new look.

## Path-traced pass, round 1 (v11-pt)

The first round on frames from Blender's Cycles (`render/pathtrace.py`), the same model and rig exported from the real-time scene. A new render path, so the count restarts. Scores 6, 5, 5, 4, 5, 5, 4: the hero's 6 is the highest any frame has scored, and for the first time no critic asked for penumbra, bounce or contact occlusion as such. What every report said instead was the same two things. The fill swamped the key: the dome I had given Cycles was about five times the real-time hemisphere once units are counted properly (an irradiance is π times a dome radiance), so every face sat at one value and nothing sat on the floor. And the lacquer had nothing to reflect: a uniform grey dome, razor arrises, a studio with a hard horizon seam. Two things were plainly bugs: the sun wash high on the hero's wall was the sun spilling over a window wall with no ceiling, and the black band under the crate was its base board sharing the walls' outer planes, which a path tracer renders black.

v12 answers all of that without touching the product: the three rooms closed with a ceiling and two more walls, so daylight is only what the window admits; light units converted (sun as irradiance, area lamps as radiance times area times π, points times 4π, the dome divided by π); the studio floor sweeping up into a backdrop, its key a softbox and its dome a gradient; the bronze rendered as a metal; the cabinet's arrises eased by about a millimetre in the shader, silhouette unchanged; the crate base inset. Three critic notes are not critic fixes and go to the owner: the spout reading as a lens barrel (02b), the crate wall hiding the drivers (06), and the chair as a massing model (02a) if the room is to be dressed.

## Path-traced pass, round 2 (v12-pt)

Scores 5, 6, 5, 5, 5, 6, 5 against round 1's 6, 5, 5, 4, 5, 5, 4: the studio and the crate up, the hero down a point. The v12 changes did what they were meant to (nobody asked for a cove, a horizon or a black band again, and the back's bronze is now read as a plate rather than kraft) but they exposed the next layer, and the reports agree on it. In the rooms, the sun lit the faces the camera could not see: the windows were on the left and the cameras on the right, so each cabinet's own shadow fell behind it and the hero's two cabinets stood one in sun and one in shade, "two colourways in one shot". In the studio the dome was still the loudest source, so the key had no direction to name and the plinths sat on the floor without a contact line. The close-up's key was too big and too far to put a gradient on a panel that fills the frame. The old room's lamp was tungsten amber on a white product.

One bug surfaced on the way: glTF marks the sky planes unlit, and Blender's importer builds that as emission for camera rays only, transparent to every other ray, so the closed rooms had no sky light at all until the planes were rebuilt as plain emitters (the first v12 hero came out as a cave; it was discarded before the critic saw it).

v13, the third and last round for these shots: the windows move to the right wall in all three rooms, the camera's side, so the sun lights the faces the camera sees and the shadows fall where it can see them, the hero's window 2.6 m wide so both cabinets stand in one band; the chairs move out of those shadows. The studio dome drops to fill strength and the softbox sits where the real-time key stood (closer and smaller for the rake), the back's key rakes from the upper right. The old room's window turns to a cool dusk against a warm-white lamp. Paper cones go matte, plank grain and gaps stronger, the eased arris 2 mm, the desk's fill cooler. Still spec, not render, and still with the owner: the crate wall hiding the pints' drivers, the spout reading as a lens at room distance, the Chocolate wordmark as polished metal on a cream plinth, and the close-up's camera showing only the gable's lip.

## Path-traced pass, round 3 (v13-pt): the cap

Scores 5, 4, 4, 5, 5, 5, 5. Three rounds on the path-traced look have now been run on every shot, so this is the last word on these frames in this batch. The arc: round 1 (6, 5, 5, 4, 5, 5, 4) found a dome that swamped the key and nothing for the lacquer to reflect; round 2 (5, 6, 5, 5, 5, 6, 5) found the sun on the faces the camera could not see and the studio still dome-lit; round 3 found, with the windows moved to the camera's side and the studio keys raked, the next layer down, and it is a plateau rather than a slope. Each round fixed what the one before named and the fresh critic found the same score's worth of new things.

What round 3 names, and what it would take:

- **Exposure, both ways.** In the rooms the white body sits on the shoulder (231 on both faces of the apartment's cabinet, so the faces do not separate); in the studio it sits at 189 with nothing above 204. The real-time exposures came across unchanged and AgX places white differently from ACES. A per-room exposure in the path tracer: two-thirds of a stop down in the apartment, half a stop up in the studio.
- **The sun's footprint.** With the window at 14 to 24 degrees of elevation the band is long and narrow, so a cabinet stands at its edge and its shadow leaves the patch; the hero's band climbs the wall as a rectangle the critic read as painted on. Raise the sun, whiten it so the floor stops going orange, push the band a metre back.
- **Contact and weight.** Big soft fills (the sky through a 2.6 m window, the studio dome even at fill strength) lift the contact line under every base. Halve the studio dome again and darken its backdrop a stop; in the rooms the fix is the exposure above.
- **The set.** The chair is a massing model, the floors are flat fills with hairline seams, the desk is the crate's own colour. A chair that is furniture, a darker desk top, grain that reads at room distance.
- **Beyond render, for the owner.** The spout reads as a lens barrel from across a room (every round, both rooms). The crate wall hides the pints' drivers. The Chocolate wordmark is polished metal on a cream plinth. The tweeter sits in the bowl with no visible flange. The close-up's camera shows only the gable's lip. The old room's lamp lights the product from out of frame. None of these is a critic fix.

Where it stands: the path-traced frames are the best this project has made, and no critic in three rounds asked for penumbra, bounce or a horizon again; what they ask for now is a photographer's second day on set. The real-time frames remain the layout check and the canvas the source of truth for layout, copy and proportions.

### 2026-10-05: the owner's refinement pass (v14)

The path-traced look had used its three rounds per shot (v11 to v13). The owner asked for a further pass ("more photorealistic, polish the design, make sure the milk crate version is correct"), so the v14 batch counts as a new batch: round 1 again, at most three.

Where it ended (v16, round 3, the last in this batch): 01 5, 02a 5, 02b 4, 03 4, 04 4, 05 5, 06 5 (round 1 was 4 to 5). The rounds found three model bugs that every earlier batch had shipped: dust caps squashed across their axis, a port flange modelled as a solid disc over the bore, and cabinet corners the shader's bevel never reached because each panel was its own mesh. The owner's questions the critics raised are in the rows: the letter metal on light plinths, Chocolate's pale throat, a lip or trim at the bowl's mouth, shot 04's straight-on brief, the woofer's place near the plinth, a reveal where the gable meets the body.

### 2026-10-06: the owner's design points (v17)

The refinement batch had used its three rounds (v14 to v16). The owner then made four of the design points its critics raised (dark letters on the light plinths, a polished lip at the scoop, a three-quarter back view, Chocolate's throat run dark) and asked for an update based on the critics, so v17 is a new batch: round 1 again, at most three. v17 also carries round 3's render notes (the hero's sun on the fronts; the bright room's beam on the cabinet; a window patch in the older room; the close-up wider, its top light a strip, a card into the bowl; the line-up's key further round; the crate's fill down). Shot 04 now has two frames, a (straight on) and b (three-quarter, new).

Where it ended (v19, round 3, the last in this batch): 01 5, 02a 5, 02b 4, 03 5, 04b 5, 06 5; 04a stopped at round 1 (4) and 05 at round 2 (5), since what their critics asked for (a three-quarter camera) is what their briefs rule out. Round 1 was 4 to 5. The rounds found two real faults: a grey ring round every driver (the shader's eased edge rounding each hole's rim; v18 tucks the holes under the flanges) and the pints' edges eased at the floorstander's 6 mm, which made them look inflated (fixed in v20, rendered after the batch's last round). The owner's questions they raised are in the README's open questions: a reveal where the gable meets the body, whether the arrises may round the silhouette, taller crate windows, dark letters on the back, a narrower Facts table; and the tweeter, which still reads as an eye from off its axis.

### 2026-10-07: the Nutrition Facts printed on the body (v20 of the back)

The owner moved the Facts off the bronze plate and onto the body, printed under the clear like a carton's panel. A change to the printed look, so the back starts a new count: round 1 again, at most three. The other shots are unchanged by it. From this batch on, each image goes to its critic under a neutral file name, as `.claude/skills/critic-round` says; in the v17 batch's round 3 the critics were given the repo paths, which carry the product's name.

### 2026-10-07: the owner's geometry (v21 on)

The owner asked what it would take to reach 10/10 and chose three geometry changes: no lip at the scoop, the tweeter's ring and seat moulded black; the cabinet's vertical corners and the gable's hips rounded 3 mm in the model (the fin's edges 1.5); a 3 mm shadow line where the gable meets the body, like the plinth's. Then OPEN OTHER SIDE moved onto the fin's back face and the back's letters centred between the edge and the Facts. A new look, so a new count: round 1 again, at most three. Round 1 is every shot at v19's light and camera with the new geometry (v21); round 2 (v22) is light and composition per shot; round 3 is finishing. Every image goes to its critic under a neutral file name.

Where it ended (v23, round 3, the last in this batch): every frame at 5 but the crate at 4; 02b reached 6 at round 2 and fell back to 5. Round 1 was 4 to 5. In every shot the critics' asks converged on the same few things: edges that catch light, a finish they can name, drivers that look fitted rather than stock, and the tweeter, which from any camera off its axis sits at the right end of its scoop (it is at the back of the throat) and reads as an eye or a lens. Round 2 changed the sets and cameras; round 3 the finish: a colour coat with no gloss of its own under a full clear, edge lights seen only in reflections, AgX's very high contrast look in the studio. Found and fixed on the way: the crate's first round-2 final cut its left end (caught before its critic); the gloss roof mirrored the fin's OPEN OTHER SIDE upside down beneath it in the straight-on back (re-shot from 1.3 m; a sliver remains). At room distance the 3 mm rounds are two pixels, and no critic saw them as anything but sharp. The finish changed once more inside round 3: 03 and 04b (and a first 04a) were rendered while the colour coat still had a faint broad gloss of its own, and their edge lights lit the faces too; the rest render the colour coat matte under the clear, with the edge lights seen only in reflections (a container restart stopped the batch between the two, with nothing lost). The owner's questions from these rounds are in the README.

### 2026-10-08: the owner's answers to the critics (v24 on)

The owner answered the questions the v21 to v23 rounds raised ("address the additional critic requirements"). The changes:

- the edges rounded 6 mm (the fin's 3);
- the tweeter forward to 125 in a 74 throat, seated flush on the throat's flat floor;
- the drivers flush under satin black trim rings, the cones ribbed charcoal paper, the caps satin, the woofer 30 higher;
- the cast letters 1.5 proud with sides like their faces;
- a recessed terminal cup at 175;
- the Facts values in bold.

Two briefs changed: 04a is a straight-on detail of the label, and 05 a raised three-quarter. The critics' staging asks went in too:

- 06 on a neutral sweep;
- 02a with its sun on the speaker and the chair in the shade;
- 02b with the sun as its one main light;
- 01 with its red below AgX's shoulder;
- 03 recropped.

A new model and new briefs, so a new count: round 1 again, at most three. Every image goes to its critic under a neutral file name.

Where it ended (v26, round 3, the last in this batch): 04b and 06 at 6, 03 at 4, the other five at 5. Round 1 was 4 to 6 and round 2 all 5. What moved: the red holds its hue in every frame (Khronos PBR Neutral); the label detail lost its mirrored OPEN OTHER SIDE, the line-up its hot spots; the port reads as a tube; the crate's roofs sit a stop over their faces; the older room's silhouette reads as the carton. What did not, in any round: the white under sun at the top of the range with no falloff (each critic's fix: a real paint's albedo, about 0.8, under a satin clear); drivers that read as stock however they are mounted (flush trim rings a millimetre or two wide at room distance); the tweeter's place in its scoop from any camera above its axis (the owner's question); and the close-up and the label detail asked, every round, to be whole cartons on a floor (the briefs). Found and fixed on the way: the birch core's square corners stood through the 6 mm rounds, first at the rounds and then at the groove's corner; the denoiser drew each clear letter plane as a rectangle on a dark plinth (the planes are cut to their glyphs now); the bright room's and the hero room's left walls had no skirting (the hero room's fixed after the last render). Lost on the way: the polished clear of v26 drew the slope's light as a line and so drew the orange peel in it, crinkled vinyl to the close-up's critic (03 fell to 4) and dented foil on the crate's chocolate gable; and the corners' lights, three times as bright in the rooms in v26, also caught the fin's end rounds and read as an outline stroke. The owner's questions from this batch are in the README.

## Critic v2 on the engine (e rounds), 2026-10-08

The protocol changed (`critic/PROMPT.md` v2, CLAUDE.md): a fresh critic scores the image blind, then reads the shot
card and measures (`critic/measure.py`, by part through the render's mask) and prescribes at most eight changes, each
one setting from the card with its value now, the value to set and an accept test the next render must pass. The
engine applies them as written (`studio/engine/render.py --apply`) and `critic/round.py check` runs the tests. The
replies are in `critic/rounds/2026-10-08/`. The geometry, the colours, the drivers and the waveguide are out of scope
by the card, so each round's "out of scope" list is the record of what only the owner can change.

| Shot | Render | Round | Blind score | If fixed | What it prescribed | Next render's accept tests |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 01 hero pair | e1 | 1 | 4.5 | 7.0 | c1 the window 0.6 m toward the camera so the left unit is in the sun (its front 92 against 176); c2 the grey rug out of frame; c3 lens shift 0.12 for a copy side; c4 one glazing bar fewer (its shadow banded the fronts); c5 exposure 0.7 to 1.1; c6 a glint on the rings and surrounds; c7 the sideboard from orange to a neutral #897C6E. Out of scope: the pair's toe-in (placement), the drivers' black, the waveguide's shape. Applied all seven, and the toe-in from 12 to 5 degrees (mine: placement is not the critic's) | e2: six of seven pass (left front 230, foreground oak, copy side lum_range 21, fronts' p5 214, rings' p95 103, sideboard neutral); the fronts' median 227 against a 190 to 215 band |
